### Mathematics in the Age of AI

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 09:00–09:20
- Room: Amphitheater
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: Keynotes
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Hugo Duminil-Copin

<p>Generative artificial intelligence is transforming an ever-growing range of professions. Recently, it has succeeded in solving some of the most challenging mathematical conjectures. From software developers to artists, mathematicians and writers, no field of intellectual creativity seems beyond its reach. As AI becomes increasingly capable of automating complex tasks, it sometimes makes us lose sight of why we put so much effort into carrying them out in the first place. This talk will invite us to reflect on the role of human beings in education, research and innovation. What do we wish to preserve of our intelligence, curiosity and freedom in an age when artificial intelligence is pushing the boundaries of what is possible?</p>

### The New Rules of Marketing Online: Why Your Website Should Be Built on an AI Harness

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 09:45–10:10
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Martin Anderson-Clutz

<p>The increasingly pervasive adoption of Artificial Intelligence has left few digital experience platforms untouched. But while many are quick to add AI-driven capabilities, there's a large question we should be discussing: In an AI-first internet, how does that change what a website should be?</p>
<p>As customers increasingly begin their buying journey using AI chat interfaces and zero-click searches, the strategies for online success that have served so many brands for so long are seeing diminishing returns.</p>
<p>In this talk we'll explore a variety of ways AI will impact the nature of what constitutes success: not just for content creation, but also how and in what format it should be discoverable by LLMs. We'll also explore how AI transforms how we should think about topics like personalization, analytics, and more. We will also identify key capabilities that are imperative to succeed in this new landscape, as well as the attributes to look for in a content platform that will help you meet the moment.</p>

### Agents Without Users: Giving Every Agent Its Own Identity

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 10:15–10:40
- Room: Dragonnier 3
- Conference: ContextCon and MCP Summit
- Track: MCP in Production
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Aaron Parecki

<p>A lot of the agents going into production don't have a person sitting in front of them. They triage issues in a repository, watch a pipeline, or answer in a channel, and they get created and torn down constantly. When one of them needs to call an external API or MCP server, today's options are bad: register every agent by hand at every service, or give them all the same API key and lose any record of which agent did what.</p>
<p>The Workload Authorization Grant (WAG) is an early proposal at the IETF, which I'm co-authoring with engineers from Anthropic, OpenAI, Defakto Security and Ping Identity, to close that gap. The platform that runs the agent issues it a short-lived signed assertion naming that one agent. A service that has been configured once to trust the platform exchanges it for an access token: no per-agent registration, no long-lived secret, and every action attributable to a specific agent.</p>
<p>This talk covers the problem, how the grant works, how it's being brought into MCP as an experimental extension, and how it relates to Cross App Access, which handles agents acting for a user. This is work in progress, so I'll also be clear about what isn't settled yet: proof of possession, replay protection, and who owns an agent.</p>

### How to optimize your content for AI visibility at scale

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 10:15–10:40
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Christoph Breidert

<p>Last year at FOST Paris, we introduced a framework for evaluating content against six signals that determine whether AI systems can find, trust, and cite it — Citation Readiness, Query Alignment, Content Structure, Authority &amp; Trust, Semantic Clarity, and Technical Accessibility. This is the follow-up, and it's hands-on: how to actually build an AEO system like this in your own Drupal CMS.
We'll walk through the architecture step by step. How to model an assessment with clearly separated context fields (brand guidelines, target questions, etc.) so the AI evaluates against your organization, not generic best practice. How to structure prompts for each signal so an LLM can score reliably and consistently rather than producing vague, unusable feedback.
The hardest part of this system has nothing to do with the CMS: it's prompt engineering. AI is probabilistic, not deterministic, and getting it to produce reliable, actionable suggestions instead of generic praise or noise takes real work. We'll go through example edge that can break your prompts, explain why context engineering sometimes matters more than the prompt wording itself, and how to calibrate the right level of detail in a response — too little and the feedback is useless, too much can be percieved as AI slob.
You'll leave with a concrete blueprint for building this pattern yourself, in Drupal or any structured-content CMS, and the traps we hit along the way.</p>

### AI-enhanced is not AI-native — Rethinking CMS architecture for the agentic Era

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:00–11:25
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Artem Dmitriiev

<p>Most &quot;&quot;AI-powered CMS&quot;&quot; products today are existing systems with AI features bolted on: the human is still the operator, AI is still the assistant. But content volume, personalization demands, and the rise of AI agents as primary content consumers are breaking that model.
This talk draws on real architectural work to unpack what actually changes when AI, not a human editor, becomes the primary operator: content-as-source-of-truth over templates, governance and compliance checks built into every generation step, multi-provider AI integration (Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, self-hosted models) as a first-class requirement, and structuring content for AI agents (AIO) alongside humans.
Drupal's API-first architecture, structured content model, and open governance make it one of the strongest foundations available for AI-native systems, arguably ahead of most proprietary platforms racing to retrofit AI onto legacy cores.
The same architecture has a further advantage that few teams have yet recognized: with content and governance sitting behind Drupal’s typed API, external AI agents can act on it directly and can create or edit content under the same rules as a human editor, with no additional integration work required.In this presentation, we will share the technical challenges and considerations for preparing your CMS for brand consistency at scale, governance observability, and provider dependency in an agent-orchestrated web.</p>

### Faster at What? Using AI for Impact, Not Just Speed

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:00–11:25
- Room: Acacia 1/2
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: Enterprise AI
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Thomas Roux

<p>Faster at What? Using AI for Impact, Not Just Speed</p>
<p>AI is sold as an accelerator: more code, more pull requests, more output. But faster at what? Industry data tells a sobering story. Teams merge nearly twice as many PRs, yet review time explodes and company-level delivery barely moves. Activity is up. Impact is not.</p>
<p>In this talk, Meriem Toudjine and Thomas Roux bring two complementary lenses to the problem. Meriem takes the product side: defining direction and value with the business, and steering AI adoption toward what actually matters. Thomas takes the engineering side: treating every expected impact as a hypothesis, proving it with evidence, and using AI to shorten the loop between delivery and learning.</p>
<p>Together, they walk through a simple loop (direction, impact hypothesis, delivery, measurement, learning) and show where AI truly creates value, where it only creates noise, and how to measure the difference. You’ll leave with a practical test to tell activity from impact, and a new metric to track: how fast your organization learns, not how fast it codes.</p>

### Sovereignty Through Openness, from Domyn Edge 4B to EUROPA 1T

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:00–11:25
- Room: Amphitheater
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI to the Frontier
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Luca Antiga

<p>Sovereignty is ownership and control. At Domyn we argue for openness as the path to sovereignty, for Europe and for individual organizations. An ecosystem building on shared open foundations can achieve what no single entity can alone, while fully controlling the stack and the playbooks. This talk presents our contributions to that ecosystem.</p>
<p>Domyn Edge, state-of-the-art 4 B‑parameter model trained from scratch on European public compute, and fully open (weights, intermediate checkpoints, all data, code for training/evaluation). State‑of‑the‑art in its class, with System 1, guard, multimodal variants and on‑device deployment. EUROPA, a 1 T‑parameter MoE, now training on the JUPITER supercomputer under the EuroHPC Frontier AI Grand Challenge. Domyn Agents, an open‑source, model‑agnostic agent harness with a safety and governance‑native design.</p>
<p>In the talk we’ll cover pre/post‑training on public HPC, edge vs. frontier trade‑offs, and how an enterprise platform integrates these components for regulated sectors and acts as a flywheel for specialization.</p>

### Text and tool calls in one token stream: a hidden hard part of agents

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:00–11:25
- Room: Acacia 3/4
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI Agents in Production
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jérémie Doucy

<p>Tool calling looks solved: send tools, get JSON back. Underneath, the model streams tokens and switches to a tool call mid-stream, and each model marks that switch differently. Based on agents we run in production, this talk shows where tool calling breaks, how to test it and how to fix it without touching the model’s weights.</p>

### Let's Build, Secure, and Observe an AI Agent with an Agent Gateway

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:00–11:55
- Room: Aubépine 3
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: Workshops
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Gbadebo Bello

<p>AI agent workflows often begin as direct connections: the orchestration layer holds separate credentials, calls models and services independently, and manages tool access and reliability itself. In this hands-on workshop, you'll take an existing Banking, Fraud, and Support agent workflow and route it through a single governed layer using Fabric Gateway.</p>
<p>You'll configure the gateway to connect the workflow's LLM, MCP servers, and APIs; centralize upstream credentials; shape and progressively disclose the tool surface; and apply gateway-level controls for routing, retries, and observability. Using the accompanying comparison app, you'll see the difference between the direct and gateway-mediated paths in real time.</p>
<p>By the end, you'll understand how to move a working agent orchestration from scattered service connections to a unified, governed execution path.</p>
<p>Prerequisites: Please come expecting to participate in a hands-on workshop where you can build with your peers. Laptops open, hands on keyboards welcome!</p>

### Open Source Is Not Enough: What Makes an AI System Truly Open?

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:10–11:30
- Room: Acacia 6
- Conference: CommonsAI
- Track: Debunking session : building actual open AI is possible !
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Stefan Koehler

<p>An open model does not make an open AI system. Using PrAIvate as a practical case, this talk examines where openness can break down across data, retrieval, software, infrastructure, deployment, and governance. It asks which freedoms users actually need to inspect, reproduce, modify, move, and operate an AI system independently — and argues that openness, like sovereignty, has to be designed at system level. A short audience exercise would compare three contrasting AI system architectures and ask participants which they would consider “open”. Their responses would be used to introduce the distinctions between model openness, system openness, sovereignty, and governance.</p>

### Demo : Panoramax: example of a commons powered with and feeding computer vision

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:30–11:45
- Room: Acacia 6
- Conference: CommonsAI
- Track: Debunking session : building actual open AI is possible !
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Christian Quest, Amélie Crépin

<p>Panoramax is an open-source software stack to create street-level imagery open alternatives. Launched in 2022 by the French mapping agency (IGN) and OpenStreetMap France, it is built and maintained by a community of public entities, companies and various other contributors and now gathers over 120 million photos released under open data licenses, with a decentralized architecture allowing anyone to host an instance and join the federation. Computer vision plays a dual role here. First, as a safeguard: each photo passes through an AI-based blurring pipeline, anonymizing faces, license plates and ensuring GDPR/CNIL compliance without gatekeeping contribution. Second, as a value multiplier: the standardized (STAC) dataset is used to train and run models for tasks like road and urban furniture inventory, building diagnosis, etc. turning a commons into reusable data as detections can be shared through the semantic tagging system. Some datasets and models (e.g. road-sign detection) are published on Hugging Face. There is also potential to improve the pictures (e.g. horizontal alignment correction) and the available data represent a gold mine for the research community. This talk will show how Panoramax embodies commons governance (federation over centralization, community, open licensing) while illustrating concrete opportunities: privacy-preserving AI as an enabler of openness and derived AI use-cases as a way to sustain and multiply the value created by contributors.</p>

### AI as editor, not author: Keeping content standards consistent across hundreds of pages

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:30–11:55
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Bruno Bruno

<p>Most AI-and-content talks start with generation - the AI writes, humans clean up. This one makes the opposite case.</p>
<p>AI has pushed content production to warp speed, but oversight hasn’t kept up: on large Drupal estates, keeping standards consistent still means a senior editor reviewing hundreds of authors by hand. Slow, and impossible to scale. So instead of retroactive review, the highest-value AI doesn’t write it all - it reads, scores content against your own standards, and proposes fixes on Drupal’s open AI tooling, while editors keep authorship and the final word.</p>

### The future of robotics: from lab to real work

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 11:30–11:55
- Room: Amphitheater
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI to the Frontier
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Remi Cadene

<p>AI breaks new records every day, yet we still can't teach a robot a new task like we would teach a colleague. Rémi Cadène, co-founder and CEO of UMA, walks through the three bottlenecks standing in the way: the foundation model robots learn with, how it runs on their body, and how they deliver on real tasks. He shares the state of the art and how his team is moving it forward.</p>

### Securing AsyncAPI's CI/CD Pipelines: Lessons from Handling 2 Supply Chain Incidents

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:00–12:25
- Room: Aubépine 5
- Conference: API Standards Conference
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Florence Njeri, Lukasz Gornicki

<p>Open-source packages are now the primary attack surface used by threat actors. Rather than targeting enterprises directly, attackers are now compromising widely-used packages with a goal of reaching millions of CI/CD systems simultaneously through trusted releases.</p>
<p>Because these open-source packages form the backbone of modern enterprise workflows, including cloud pipelines, CI/CD systems, and developer tooling, attackers are increasingly targeting open-source organizations to poison these packages and publish them to trusted registries such as npm and the VS Code Marketplace, compromising hundreds of downstream organizations simultaneously through trusted releases installed by their developers.</p>
<p>Over the past year, AsyncAPI faced two supply chain attacks by <strong>TeamPCP</strong> and <strong>Miasma</strong>, compromising packages with up to 4M+ combined weekly downloads, such as:</p>
<ul>
<li>@asyncapi/specs (4M+ weekly)</li>
<li>@asyncapi/generator (~130k weekly)</li>
<li>Official VS Code extensions</li>
</ul>
<p>This session presents the tactics, techniques, and procedures (TTPs) we saw used in both incidents. We will trace how a single compromised release propagates into enterprise and public sector environments across Europe and France.</p>
<p>Finally, attendees will gain actionable defense strategies and tools we have been using to tighten our security measures. We will showcase how we overhauled our release pipelines using:</p>
<ul>
<li>OIDC Trusted Publishing</li>
<li>Automated CI/CD security scanning of every workflow change before it can release</li>
<li>Hardened token isolation</li>
<li>SHA-pinned GitHub Actions</li>
<li>Least-privilege permission scopes on automation bots</li>
</ul>
<p>Attendees will leave with practical resources and tools to audit their own GitHub Actions, build pipelines, and IDE extension supply chains against active threat groups.</p>
<p><strong>The Technical Incidents:</strong></p>
<p>This section of the talk will provides first-hand incident response account from the AsyncAPI Initiative security team following two real-world software supply-chain compromises orchestrated by threat groups TeamPCP and Miasma.</p>
<ul>
<li>Exploitation of GitHub Actions pull_request_target workflows (pwn requests) to steal elevated repository secrets.</li>
<li>Spam pull requests used to hide the real attack in the workflow logs, and a docs PR using an MDX file to run attacker code in our build pipeline.</li>
<li>How the attackers released compromised packages with trusted credentials releasing malicious builds of @asyncapi/specs, @asyncapi/generator, and developer VS Code extensions directly to npm and the VS Code Marketplace, including one release through our own OIDC pipeline.</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Direct Impact of Supply Chain Attacks for Financial Sector Attendees in France and the EU:</strong></p>
<p>For banking, fintech, and public sector ecosystems in France and the EU operating under strict EU regulatory frameworks like DORA and NIS2, open-source packages and developer extensions represent an unmonitored third-party dependency vector. A poisoned upstream library imported into an internal financial pipeline or a developer’s IDE circumvents traditional perimeter controls.</p>
<p><strong>Actionable Takeaways:</strong></p>
<p>Attendees will leave with practical blueprints we implemented to remediate and harden our release infrastructure and further insights on how to protect their organizations from being compromised through supply chain attacks.</p>

### The AI Safety Net: Let AI Coordinate Changes Across Your Site Without Risking Production

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:00–12:25
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Fabian Franz

<p>What would you let an AI agent change on your Drupal site if none of it reached your users until you were ready?</p>
<p>When you give AI full freedom to rewrite pages, populate sections, and coordinate changes across your whole site, then there is one catch. Whatever it builds can be visible to users the moment it finishes. That limitation is what should stop teams from putting AI near production.</p>
<p>Workspaces solves this. Everything inside is safe, reversible, and self-contained, because the complete result remains contained in the Workspace. There it can be reviewed, revised, or discarded before anyone publishes it.</p>
<p>Build the next version of your site with AI while the published site stays exactly as it is. When a generation fails, nothing breaks for your users and nothing is permanent until you say so.</p>
<p>Fabian Franz, one of the original architects of Workspaces and Tag1’s VP of Engineering, will demonstrate this live. Watch an AI agent reshape content across a Workspace and see the one deliberate step that puts those changes on your live site.</p>
<p>Come see what it looks like to build a Drupal site with AI and Workspaces.</p>
<p>What will you create first?</p>

### Finding Your Team’s Rhythm: Turn Individual AI Wins into Team Workflows

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:00–12:55
- Room: Aubépine 4
- Conference: Marketing Reset
- Track: Workshops
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Nate Holmes

<p>Most marketing teams already use AI. The problem is that everyone uses it alone. One person builds a great blog prompt, another builds a better one, and a third has never seen either. Each person is faster at the old process, but the team is not faster together. Output is inconsistent, knowledge stays in silos, and leaders cannot govern what they cannot see.
At Acquia, our marketing team spent the past year changing that. We turned scattered prompts into a shared library of more than 60 reusable AI skills, built a single source of truth that both people and AI can use, and put governance directly into the workflow. The results included a 21% quarter-over-quarter increase in AI citations and about two hours saved on every blog we
publish.
This is not a case study you sit back and listen to. It is a working session. In 50 minutes, attendees work through the same four moves our team made, applied to their own organization.
The four moves:
● Sound check: Audit your content source of truth. Is your brand voice, persona, proof point, and claims content structured so AI can use it, and does each piece have an owner?
● Write the setlist: Pick one workflow your team runs at least three times a month and design it as a reusable AI skill, defining its trigger, intake questions, sources, steps, tools, and checks.</p>
<p>● Gate the show: Map where people approve, where AI drafts, and where AI checks, so governance is part of the process.
● Plan rehearsal: Build a 30-day upskilling plan covering executive support, protected practice time, a shared space for skills, and named AI champions.
Attendees will leave with four artifacts:
● A source-of-truth list
● A skill design for one real workflow
● A governance map
● A 30-day team rehearsal plan
This workshop is for marketing and digital operations leaders who have seen individual AI gains and now need to scale them across a team without losing control of brand, accuracy, or quality.</p>

### Local AI as a Commons: repurposing hardware for neighborhood AI services

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:15–12:30
- Room: Acacia 6
- Conference: CommonsAI
- Track: Working with AI to build digital commons: lessons learned from communities
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Bruno Caldas Vianna

<p>This presentation describes how a Barcelona neighborhood repurposed discarded hardware into a self-managed AI server - offering translation, digital literacy, and community-driven tools - challenging corporate AI monopolies. Its fragility (manual backups, volunteer labor) exposes tensions between sovereignty and convenience, while its Catalan-language focus disrupts colonial digital access. These characteristics create friction with users accustomed to seamless, corporate AI platforms. While some embrace the server’s sovereignty, others resist its fragility, preferring the convenience of ChatGPT or Gemini, exposing tensions between collective agency and individual convenience.  This first-person account explores how precarious infrastructure can foster responsible AI through collective control, questioning: How do marginalized communities reclaim AI’s public values while navigating technical and political precarity? We will share usage data and trends as results of the experience, as well as personal accounts from users. This information and the session feedback will allow us to continue the development of the server and the research afforded by it.</p>

### AI model for Maps

- Date: 1 December 2026

### Feedback on AI in a Knowledge Commons

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:30–12:45
- Room: Acacia 6
- Conference: CommonsAI
- Track: Working with AI to build digital commons: lessons learned from communities
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jérémy Perret, Charlie Vernot-Rochey

<p>Coexiscience is developing DocCommuns in collaboration with partner organizations in the social and solidarity economy (SSE): a tool that allows a collective to annotate its own documents according to criteria it defines, either manually or using AI. Its use has raised questions that go beyond the scope of AI. Is an AI-generated annotation the responsibility of the person who requested it, or is the group responsible for the AI it uses? Who is responsible for choosing which documents to analyze? Moreover, is there even a single person in charge? Who has the right to write the criteria? Who validates them? And what exactly do we want to annotate? When an AI can annotate in one hour what a collective used to take a year to read, who still has the time to verify it?  We believe these governance issues are not new. The use of AI primarily shatters the unspoken agreements that allowed us to avoid asking them in the first place. By increasing the scale and diversity of changes that anyone can make to shared resources, it forces us to view the use of shared tools as a matter of collective decision-making: who sees what, who acts on what, and who is accountable for what.  Proposed participatory activity: In small groups, each participant will identify a problem within their organization raised by the use of AI; then we’ll brainstorm together the collective decisions needed to address it.</p>

### Make Your API Metadata AI-Ready and Automatically Discoverable by Agents

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:30–12:55
- Room: Aubépine 5
- Conference: API Standards Conference
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Galina Tuova

<p>Open Resource Discovery (ORD) addresses this with an open standard — governed by the Linux Foundation (NeoNephos Project) — for decentralized, self-describing systems. Instead of registering everything in a central catalog by hand, systems publish machine-readable metadata at the source, so catalogs, tools, and AI agents can crawl and refresh it automatically from a single entry point. ORD complements OpenAPI and AsyncAPI rather than replacing them, adding the high-level context and relationships that make APIs — along with events, data products, and AI resources such as agents, skills, and workflows — automatically discoverable, together with their contracts and documentation.</p>
<p>ORD is also built for AI consumers: dedicated AI-facing metadata (such as hints for LLMs and agent orchestrators) and native support for agent protocols let AI orchestrators dynamically discover the capabilities available to them in a system landscape.</p>
<p>With ORD, organizations can publish their metadata offerings, analyze their IT landscape in real time, and build platforms for software development, data management, and AI.</p>

### Swisscheese: the missing review process of your AI software factory

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 12:30–12:55
- Room: Acacia 1/2
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: Enterprise AI
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Djalel Benbouzid

<p>AI software factories are everywhere right now. They are also the logical next step: models can now carry out long-running tasks, and agent harnesses have matured enough to run many of them in parallel. But models still make unpredictable mistakes, and harnesses are deliberately built to be as generic as possible. Scaling from a couple of Claude Code terminals to a real agent fleet multiplies both problems.</p>
<p>Software engineering, and engineering in general, has dealt with unreliable components before. One of its lessons is the Swiss cheese model of risk management: no single layer of defence is perfect, so you stack several whose holes don't line up. In this talk, we apply that model to coding agents. We rethink parts of the harness around what current models do well and where they fail, and end up with a package that scales agent-based code review massively and uses agents' ability to supervise and correct each other. The result: Swiss cheese software.</p>

### Defending the commons is not enough: new strategies for AI knowledge loops

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:20
- Room: Acacia 6
- Conference: CommonsAI
- Track: The "dark commons" and countermoves
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Alek Tarkowski, Ilario Valdelli

<p>Open Future and Wikimedia CH have worked over the past year on developing a strategy for the knowledge commons in the age of AI. This resulted with the white paper &quot;Wikimedia, the commons, and the new AI knowledge loop&quot; (2026). With the keynote, we want to present the mission for knowledge commons and AI outlined in the paper, and build engagement with this mission.   We argue that lnowledge commons have been sustained by knowledge loops: reciprocal flows of content, attention and resources between commons and dominant platforms. The rapid emergence of AI breaks this reciprocity, in well documented cases of extraction of value. The &quot;dark commons&quot; is a specific failure mode, where it exists as an invisible substrate feeding proprietary systems.   Since openness alone no longer secures the health of the commons, we propose three indicators (openness, sustainability, value generation), three types of value flows (extractive, reciprocal, generative) and three postures (defensive closure, passive acceptance, active stance). With our mission, we argue for a generative, active stance that requires stewards of the commons to engage in AI model and interface development: a mission aligned with the vision of public AI.  Our proposal for the participatory session: participants present strategies their communities are adopting. We map these collectively onto the matrix, and identify concrete moves that shift projects towards more generative postures.</p>

### Agents Without Users: Giving Every Agent Its Own Identity

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:25
- Room: Baobab 3
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI Agents in Production
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Aaron Parecki

<p>A lot of the agents going into production don't have a person sitting in front of them. They triage issues in a repository, watch a pipeline, or answer in a channel, and they get created and torn down constantly. When one of them needs to call an external API or MCP server, today's options are bad: register every agent by hand at every service, or give them all the same API key and lose any record of which agent did what.</p>
<p>The Workload Authorization Grant (WAG) is an early proposal at the IETF, which I'm co-authoring with engineers from Anthropic, OpenAI, Defakto Security and Ping Identity, to close that gap. The platform that runs the agent issues it a short-lived signed assertion naming that one agent. A service that has been configured once to trust the platform exchanges it for an access token: no per-agent registration, no long-lived secret, and every action attributable to a specific agent.</p>
<p>This talk covers the problem, how the grant works, how it's being brought into MCP as an experimental extension, and how it relates to Cross App Access, which handles agents acting for a user. This is work in progress, so I'll also be clear about what isn't settled yet: proof of possession, replay protection, and who owns an agent.</p>

- Date: 1 December 2026

### Beyond Validation: Reasoning About What a Schema Means

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:25
- Room: Aubépine 5
- Conference: API Standards Conference
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jason Desrosiers

<p>Most JSON Schema implementations evaluate keywords. A validator checks a value against &quot;&quot;minimum&quot;&quot; or &quot;&quot;properties&quot;&quot; and reports whether it passed. That's enough for validation, but other questions need more. Does this schema allow anything at all? Does this &quot;&quot;minimum&quot;&quot; contradict that &quot;&quot;maximum&quot;&quot;? Is this required property one that &quot;&quot;additionalProperties: false&quot;&quot; forbids? Answering those means reasoning about what a schema means, not just evaluating it.</p>
<p>Version 2 of @hyperjump/json-schema is built around that idea. Every keyword, in every dialect from draft-04 to JSON Schema v1, lowers to a kernel: a small language of operations with a normal form. The validator runs the kernel, the optimizer rewrites it, and the output formats explain it. The linter's 48 rules read it to answer questions like the ones above. None of them keeps its own facts about keywords. What a keyword does comes from what it lowers to, so a rule written once works in every dialect. That includes custom keywords. A custom keyword is defined by what it lowers to, so the linter and optimizer understand it like a built-in one, with no extra code.</p>
<p>Understanding a schema also makes it fast. On the jsonschema-benchmark corpus, v2 validates 14 times faster than v1 and about 4 times faster than Ajv. It does that without generating code: no &quot;&quot;eval&quot;&quot; or &quot;&quot;new Function&quot;&quot;, so it runs under a strict Content Security Policy.</p>
<p>This talk shows what lowering looks like and how it makes dialects comparable. It covers how the optimizer gets speed without code generation, and how lint rules work, down to the kernel expressions they read. Attendees will leave with a way of thinking about schema semantics they can use in their own implementations and tools.</p>

### How Clients Use Drupal AI in Production While Keeping Their Data Sovereign

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:25
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Dan Lemon

<p>Drupal AI demos are everywhere, but what happens after the demo, when real editors, real compliance teams, and real content hit the system? For many organisations adopting Drupal AI, such as universities, government bodies, and enterprises, one rule came before anything shipped: their data must never be used in public AI training.</p>
<p>This session walks through real client use cases from teams we work with around the world. The stories are anonymised. Editorial teams use AI to categorise and tag thousands of pieces of content. Public sector teams rewrite complex text into simple language to meet accessibility rules. Some clients pair Drupal with our vector database to enhance their search with Search AI, so results understand meaning rather than just keywords, and answers respect who is allowed to see what. Others have replaced the traditional search button with a chatbot persona that guides visitors to the right place, whether that is the right page, a sign up, or a purchase. Agentic workflows are moving from experiments into daily routine. The common thread is that all of this runs on private, sovereign AI with zero data retention. That is what turned &quot;&quot;we are not allowed to use AI&quot;&quot; into &quot;&quot;we use AI in production every day.&quot;&quot;</p>
<p>We will explore these patterns together in a live demo built on the same sovereign stack our clients run. You will leave with a realistic picture of what Drupal AI does well in production today, and how to ship it without losing control of your data. If your organisation is still stuck at &quot;&quot;we are not allowed to use AI&quot;&quot;, this session is for you.</p>

### Running a Frontier AI Research Lab with 5 Researchers… and Agents

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:25
- Room: Acacia 5
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI for Science & Health
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jean Du Terrail

<p>It has become abundantly clear that the impressive capabilities of AI agents can be harnessed to streamline research in most scientific domains, including AI research itself, and the words &quot;&quot;recursive self-improvement (RSI)&quot;&quot; are on everyone's lips. Yet frontier labs share surprisingly few details about how to do this at scale, notably, very practically, how to organize research around this new multi-agents technology.
This talk will take you inside our biological AI model factory at Living Models where 5 AI researchers collaborate with multiple AI agents to create, train and evaluate state-of-the-art foundation models on DNA.
In this talk you'll learn, among other things, why biology is so different from other domains for AI research, why it won't be &quot;&quot;RLVR-ed&quot;&quot; (learned through reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards) as easily as code, and what we do about it at Living Models.</p>

### Let's Launch a Rocket: AI-Powered API & MCP Bootcamp

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:55
- Room: Aubépine 3
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: Workshops
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Anthony Viard

<p>With the rise of Large Language Models, AI copilots, and autonomous agents, developers can now design, test, and iterate on APIs with greater speed and granularity than ever before. Modern AI tooling makes it possible to move seamlessly from spec to collection and from collection back to code, accelerating every stage of the API lifecycle.</p>
<p>This 50-minute, hands-on bootcamp is designed to empower developers who want to build, test, and automate APIs and Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers using AI-powered workflows. You'll work in an environment where AI is always available, leveraging Postman Agent Mode to prototype, validate, and automate real, working API systems.</p>
<p>You'll move quickly from concept to execution. In this session, you will:</p>
<ul>
<li>Prototype, test, and automate API workflows using Postman Agent Mode</li>
<li>Generate environments, scripts, and documentation by describing what you need, not writing it</li>
<li>Visualize and validate live API responses as rich, interactive dashboards</li>
<li>Build, test, and validate an end-to-end integration test suite from a working API</li>
<li>Understand how AI agents interact with APIs and MCP-enabled services in practice</li>
</ul>
<p>By the end of the bootcamp, you'll have hands-on experience with a complete, end-to-end workflow for building and validating AI-ready APIs using tools and techniques you can immediately apply in your own projects.</p>
<p>Prerequisites:</p>
<ul>
<li>Please come expecting to participate in a hands-on workshop where you can build with your peers. Laptops open, hands on keyboards welcome!</li>
<li>Basic knowledge of APIs (HTTP, REST, or experience working with an API)</li>
<li>A Postman account (sign up free)</li>
<li>Postman Desktop App recommended but not mandatory</li>
<li>Familiarity with API design or testing workflows is helpful but not required</li>
<li>No prior AI or LLM experience required</li>
</ul>

### Scale your AI agents, Keep control: A Live Demo from foundation to production

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:00–14:55
- Room: Aubépine 1
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: Workshops
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Isabelle Mauny, Mohammed Riahi

<p>Every team is now building agents, and each one connects to models, tools and APIs on its own. Without a common foundation, security gaps, compliance exposure and runaway costs grow faster than the agents themselves.
This live demo takes you from the first controlled LLM connection to a governed fleet of agents running in production. Step by step, we lay the foundation and show how to add what an enterprise needs to scale:
● Security: agent identity, access control and guardrails.
● Compliance: audit trails and policy enforcement.
● Governance: visibility on which agents exist, what they call and who owns them.
● FinOps: usage tracking, quotas and cost control per team, agent and model.
You will leave with a practical blueprint to move agents from experiments to production, with control built in by design rather than added after the first incident.</p>

### Feeding the machines, starving the commons? Digital commons under AI extraction

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:20–14:40
- Room: Acacia 6
- Conference: CommonsAI
- Track: The "dark commons" and countermoves
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jean Constantin, Bastien Guerry

<p>Generative AI is built on digital commons: open datasets, open-source code, yet its rise is destabilizing the conditions sustaining them. Massive automated extraction, synthetic content pollution, infrastructural overload, and asymmetric value capture are straining the collectively governed infrastructures on which open knowledge depends. Communities respond in divergent ways: access restrictions, negotiated reciprocity through paid APIs, or renewed governance. How do digital commons adapt to ensure their sustainability under these conditions? How is the digital commons model, based on collective governance and openness, adapting to an AI-dominated internet? This keynote will be presented by VIADUCT, an Inria-led initiative focused on the data sourcing practices feeding AI development and their challenges. It will invite discussions on how digital commons can survive and shape the age of AI, through three questions: (1) What is actually breaking: are threats existential or manageable? (2) Which governance, legal, economic, and technical mechanisms can reconnect AI-scale reuse with contribution and maintenance? (3) What alliances between commons communities, public institutions, and AI actors could turn extraction into reciprocity? To answer, the keynote will present research evidences from interviews with a wide variety of digital common initiatives as well as a systematic analysis of AI-related policies enacted by commons.</p>

### Can agents learn from their mistakes?

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 14:30–14:55
- Room: Baobab 3
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI Agents in Production
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jules Belveze

<p>Agents make mistakes. The frustrating part is watching them make the same ones again. What would it take for an agent to learn from a failed run? Agents can use past runs to revise their instructions, tools, or memory without retraining the model. Recent research explores different ways to turn that experience into useful changes. The tricky part is checking whether a change actually helps. It might fix one case and break another, or improve a score while leaving the underlying problem untouched. Learning from mistakes means testing proposed fixes and keeping evidence of what worked, what failed, and why.</p>

<p>Arazzo changes the unit of exchange. By composing API operations into business-defined workflows with typed inputs, success criteria and structured failure handling, Arazzo lets organisations expose what agents actually need: goal-oriented capabilities, not a catalogue of primitives. Those workflows become first-class MCP tools — derived directly from the Arazzo document, with no hard-coded tool definitions to write or lifecycle to manage. Descriptions, typed schemas and examples are generated from the workflow itself, reducing search-and-execute overhead and guiding agents toward completion rather than exploration.</p>

### Adopting AI at the European Commission: Sovereign, zero-hallucination AI across 24 languages

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:00–15:25
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Frederik Wouters

<p>Most organisations treating Drupal as a CMS are sitting on an untapped advantage. EPSO and Dropsolid discovered it the hard way: when you need to deploy production-grade AI in a regulated institutional environment with full data sovereignty, auditability and zero tolerance for unverifiable answers, Drupal's API-first architecture and content modeling capabilities turn out to be exactly the right foundation. Not despite being a CMS but because of what lies underneath it.</p>
<p>This session tells the story of how the European Personnel Selection Office deployed a sovereign AI Answer Engine serving tens of thousands of recruitment candidates across all 24 official EU languages, with every answer cited back to the exact source document and correctness enforced at the model gateway level. No commercial AI platform. No proprietary lock-in. Built on Drupal, Mistral and a RAG pipeline running entirely on EU-sovereign infrastructure.</p>
<p>We will walk through how Drupal functions as the operational control plane: managing content ingestion and chunking, orchestrating the vector retrieval pipeline, exposing structured data to the language model and rendering cited responses back to users: all through a single administrative interface that EPSO's own team operates and owns. We will show why Drupal's content modeling made multilingual RAG tractable across 24 languages and how its API layer served as the bridge between institutional knowledge and AI inference.</p>

### Beyond the Broker: What Event Gateways Are Really Trying to Solve

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:00–15:25
- Room: Aubépine 5
- Conference: API Standards Conference
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Daniel Kocot

<p>Event gateways are becoming a popular answer to a real problem in event-driven architecture: the lack of a clear control plane.
But they should not be understood as API gateways for events. Events have different failure modes. Ownership is less visible. Compatibility changes over time. Policies are harder to enforce without coupling producers and consumers again. Observability depends on more than traffic flowing through a single point.
This talk examines where event gateways are useful, where brokers, schema registries, catalogs, and portals already cover part of the problem, and where the gateway metaphor starts to break down.
The focus is not another infrastructure layer. The focus is which governance capabilities event-driven systems actually need, and how AsyncAPI can help describe them.</p>

### People Decide, Agents Deliver: Governed AI for Digital Experience with Acquia Source

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:00–15:55
- Room: Aubépine 4
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Track: Workshops
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Martin Anderson-Clutz

<p>AI has changed how people find information, and marketing teams are under pressure to create more content without losing control of their brand. Adding more AI tools often makes that harder, not easier.
This session introduces Acquia Source, the agentic content platform that teams can start using today. Acquia AI agents plan the work, bring the plan to a person for approval, and then deliver it across web governance, ROI and insights, SEO and AEO, experimentation, search, and personalization. That work draws on governed content and assets in Source CMS and Acquia DAM, both of which connect to other AI tools through MCP. Underneath it all sit Acquia Cloud Platform and Drupal, so teams get security, scale, and extensibility without vendor lock-in.
Through short demos and a real-world case study, a WordPress-to-Drupal migration automated with AI tools by Acquia partner FreelyGive, attendees see what governed, agentic content operations look like in practice.</p>
<p>Attendees will learn:
● How a plan, approve, deliver pattern keeps people in control of AI agents
● How one request can draw on several capabilities, such as SEO and accessibility, under a single approval
● How MCP lets external AI tools work with governed content and assets
● What an AI-automated migration to Drupal involved, and what it means for teams considering a move</p>

### Twake.ai: When the Sovereign Digital Workspace Becomes Agentic

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:00–15:55
- Room: Aubépine 7
- Conference: Digital Resilience Europe
- Track: Workshops
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Michel-Marie Maudet

<p>For last years, the &quot;European alternative to the US digital workplace&quot; was a slide in a presentation, not a product you could install. That has changed.</p>
<p>Twake.ai is an open source, AI-first collaborative suite: mail, chat, drive, documents and meetings. It now runs in production at a scale that answers the question. In France, ANFSI uses it for the internal security forces. The governments of Mauritius and Mongolia have adopted it as national infrastructure. And through LINAGORA's open source framework agreement with CANUT, every French local authority, health service, school can now order it and migrate through a standard public purchasing channel. This removes the procurement barrier that stopped most sovereignty projects before they even started.</p>
<p>The second half of the talk looks forward. We are making Twake.ai agentic. Agents work across mail, documents, tasks and business systems inside the customer's own tenant, under the organization's own rules, on European models. This is not one more AI assistant in your inbox. It is a move from the augmented user to the orchestrated organization, and it is the first time a sovereign stack can lead instead of follow.</p>
<p>I will share real numbers, real limits, and what we provide today to our customers.</p>

### A tale of two bills: scaling a safety-critical AI health assistant

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:30–15:55
- Room: Acacia 5
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI for Science & Health
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Axel Colin De Verdière

<p>Doctolib’s AI health assistant already talks to half a million patients, with capabilities built by several teams across the company. Our target is millions of users, dozens of engineers and dozens of capabilities. In a patient-facing product, safety does not scale for free.
In this talk, we share what we have learned halfway there. Scaling comes with two bills. The runtime bill grows with users: each architecture choice, from MCP servers to direct API calls and sub-agents, changes the cost and latency of every conversation. The safety bill grows with engineers and users: we constantly improve our safety with a loop because no human can read every conversation. It relies on expert feedback on sampled conversations, regression tests and fixes, and these tests then protect future changes. These additional costs becomes a merge &amp; deploy gates that is expensive, slow and couples teams together... but still necessary.
You will leave with real orders of magnitude, the trade-offs we made, and the problems we have not solved yet.</p>

### AI Assisted Medical Research Reviews with Drupal

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:30–15:55
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Vijay Mani

<p>The Clinical Practice Research Datalink (CPRD) provisions anonymized clinical data to research institutes and companies worldwide via its electronic Research Application Portal. Because of the sensitive nature of primary care data and linked datasets, every research application undergoes a rigorous, multi-stage review process involving administrative screening, Information Governance, lay reviewers, and subject-matter experts.</p>
<p>This case study explores how we integrated a standalone &quot;&quot;AI Reviewer Service&quot;&quot; into the  Drupal portal to augment this highly complex, human-in-the-loop workflow.</p>
<p>By building an API-first bridge between our Drupal platform and an Node.js AI service powered by the AI SDK, we created an intelligent assistant capable of validating incoming research applications. The AI evaluates applications against strict domain constraints.</p>
<p>In this session, we will share the practical reality of making open-source AI integration work in a highly regulated environment. We will cover:</p>
<p>APIs for AI : How we structured and served complex Drupal application data (including multi-study licenses, dataset requests, and protocol details) to an external AI agent.</p>
<p>Human-in-the-Loop Workflows : The UX of integrating AI inside the CMS so that it assists, rather than replaces, the human review hierarchy.</p>
<p>Structuring Domain Knowledge : Techniques for grounding the LLM in complex business logic, such as CPRD's strict rules around data usage, datasets.</p>
<p>Governance &amp; Trust : How we ensure AI-assisted outputs remain transparent and trustworthy in the context of healthcare data provisioning.</p>

### From Discovery to API with AI

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:30–15:55
- Room: Acacia 3/4
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: Open European Models
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Mike Amundsen

<p>AI-native development is changing how we design and build APIs. As AI makes code generation faster and less expensive, the constraint shifts toward design: how systems are defined, aligned, and governed over time. This talk presents a practical approach to AI-native API design, using AI as a collaborator that augments human expertise and strengthens design
capabilities.
Starting with domain discovery, you'll see how AI helps establish a shared vocabulary and carries it through OpenAPI definitions, documentation, security profiles, and test scripts, culminating in a working prototype. Throughout the end-to-end example, the focus is on traceability: maintaining a clear, reviewable connection between business intent and running systems while developing the skills needed to design effective APIs.
For architects and decision-makers, this approach offers a consistent, reviewable process with measurable outcomes. For developers, it reduces manual effort while strengthening core design skills. The result is an AI-native development process that accelerates delivery, preserves human oversight, and supports long-term API governance.</p>

### Vibe-coding in the enterprise: what your platform has to enforce

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 15:30–15:55
- Room: Acacia 1/2
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: Enterprise AI
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Ruben Fiszel

<p>AI agents can now build internal tools, workflows and apps in minutes, and business teams are using them whether IT approves or not. Generating the code is no longer the hard part. The hard part is what comes next: what the app is allowed to access, who reviews it, how it reaches production, and who owns it a year later. This talk covers the principles we've seen hold up across enterprise deployments: keep everything as code, isolate experiments in forks, apply least privilege to authors, agents and running apps, sort review by blast radius, and treat ownership as a first-class concern. We end with what this looks like when the platform enforces it by default.</p>

### Not Yet Valid: Four Questions Your Editor Asks a JSON Schema

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 16:30–16:55
- Room: Aubépine 5
- Conference: API Standards Conference
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Bart Dubbeldam

<p>A schema answers the question: is this finished document valid? However, an editor often sees an unfinished document. While someone types, the file is not yet valid, and the editor still has to answer four questions:</p>
<ol>
<li>Where am I in a half-typed, broken document?</li>
<li>What could go here?</li>
<li>What actually went wrong?</li>
<li>What does this part of the schema mean?</li>
</ol>
<p>This talk works through each question using the JSON Schema support behind hover, completion, go-to-definition and diagnostics in KSON, an open-source configuration language.</p>
<p>Some examples: an <code>if</code> that depends on a field you haven't typed yet. A single typo in a <code>oneOf</code> that gives you an error for every branch, even though <code>const</code> and <code>enum</code> could tell the editor which branch you meant. A property with three descriptions, and no rule for which one hover shows.</p>
<p>Each section ends with a rule schema authors can apply today.</p>

### Open Source knowledge management in the age of AI agents

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 16:30–16:55
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: DrupalAI Summit
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Wolfgang Ziegler

<p>AI agents are becoming part of the workforce, and accurate, up-to-date knowledge is what makes them powerful. Get it right and agents deliver expert work; get it wrong and errors come back as facts, complete with a citation. Most teams feel the problem already: knowledge scattered across tools, nobody sure what is still current, and people and their agents now producing updates faster than anyone can review.</p>
<p>We introduce a workflow that works: People and AI agents work side by side in one place, with approval that always stays with people. We show the supporting tooling: Markdown-first content, MCP built in, all open source and complemented with AI that keeps the knowledge consistent as it grows.</p>
<p>Knowledge management is shifting from productivity tooling to strategic AI infrastructure. The companies that rethink it now will get the most out of their AI agents in the years ahead.</p>

### Panel : Vers des IA de recommandation démocratiques – Le projet Tournesol

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 16:30–16:55
- Room: Acacia 1/2
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI, Democracy & Society
- Type: Panel discussion
- Speakers: Jean Lou Fourquet, Lê Nguyên Hoang

<p>Chaque jour, des milliards de personnes voient leur vision du monde façonnée par des algorithmes opaques qui décident de ce qu’elles voient en ligne. Ces intelligences artificielles de recommandation — moteurs invisibles de plateformes comme YouTube, TikTok ou Instagram — influencent profondément le débat public, l’accès à l’information et même la stabilité démocratique.
Le projet Tournesol est né de trois constats fondamentaux :</p>
<pre><code>1) Les systèmes de recommandation, tels qu’ils existent aujourd’hui, ont tendance à fragiliser nos démocraties.

2) Ces systèmes doivent être véritablement régulés et les plateformes qui les développent redevables.

3) Construire un espace numérique plus démocratique est un défi existentiel pour nos sociétés.
</code></pre>
<p>Tournesol développe des systèmes de recommandation alternatifs et plus démocratiques, fondés sur les jugements et les intérêts collectifs des citoyens. Le projet recueille et agrège des évaluations humaines (volitions) — des jugements portés par de vraies personnes sur la qualité, la fiabilité et la valeur sociétale des vidéos YouTube, en les comparant entre elles. Entièrement open source, publiquement auditable, et guidé par des valeurs collectives plutôt que par des incitations commerciales, la plateforme Tournesol repose sur quatre piliers essentiels d'un numérique démocratique :</p>
<pre><code>1) Transparence – Les systèmes de recommandation doivent être ouverts, vérifiables et compréhensibles par le plus grand nombre. Les algorithmes et les données de Tournesol sont publiquement accessiblesc, permettant aux citoyens et aux chercheurs de comprendre comment les évalutations sont faites.

2) Résilience face aux attaques – Les plateformes et leurs IA de recommandation doivent être protégées contre les manipulations massives menées par attaques concertées d'un très grand nombre de faux comptes. Tournesol intègre des mécanismes de détection et de résistance à ces menaces, garantissant une participation authentique.

3) Basé sur des avis réfléchis – Les recommandations devraient refléter les choix délibérés des utilisateurs plutôt que leurs réactions impulsives (clics, balayages, etc.). Tournesol privilégie l’évaluation volontaire et réfléchie aux signaux de préférence instantanés, favorisant une culture de la réflexion plutôt que de l’addiction.

4) Favorable à l'émergence d'un socle commun informationnel – Les espaces numériques devraient favoriser une compréhension commune du monde, plutôt que de fragmenter les utilisateurs dans des bulles hyper-personnalisées. Tournesol encourage l’exposition collective à des contenus de qualité, contribuant à reconstruire un socle de connaissances partagé.
</code></pre>
<p>Au cours de la présentation de tournesol.app et de ses extensions, les participants découvriront :</p>
<pre><code>* Ce à quoi pourrait ressembler une gouvernance collaborative de la recommandation par l’IA ;

* Comment Tournesol peut transformer leur expérience de YouTube ;

* Comment le projet applique les quatre piliers d’un espace numérique démocratique, à l’inverse des grandes plateformes comme TikTok ;

* Et quelles sont les prochaines étapes du projet pour renforcer encore son alignement avec ces principes.
</code></pre>
<p>En définitive, Tournesol invite chacun à repenser la manière dont les algorithmes façonnent notre monde numérique partagé — non pas en simples consommateurs, mais en citoyens actifs.</p>

<p>In January 2026, Aleph Alpha’s research team started building a new training pipeline. ### Guerillia: how can we take back power over artificial intelligence?

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 17:00–17:25
- Room: Acacia 1/2
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: AI, Democracy & Society
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Christophe Payet

<p>Artificial intelligence is everywhere. Are we doomed to depend on Big Tech? Are there other ways to make these tools our own? Led by Christophe Payet, journalist and essayist, co-creator of PhantasIA on Arte and author of GuerillIA. Mener la bataille culturelle de l’intelligence artificielle (Éditions du Seuil), this talk goes beyond critique to ask what truly reclaiming AI could mean. How can we adopt other tools, uses and imaginaries? Against the myth of “superintelligence,” artists, activists and hackers are already diverting these technologies and inventing other ways to conceive and use them. Caught between blissful technosolutionism and a growing, wholesale rejection, this is a critical yet enthusiastic invitation to make AI a genuine tool for emancipation.</p>

### The Practical AI Starter Kit: Low-Risk, High-Impact Wins You Can Launch Today

- Date: 1 December 2026

- Conference: DrupalAI Summit

<p>Drupal's structured architecture and community ecosystem makes it the perfect CMS for AI Agents to use both inside Drupal and outside Drupal. Since the launch of ChatGPT the community has come together with an AI module, an AI Initiative of agency owners and now &quot;&quot;AI Best Practises&quot;&quot;, a collection of tools to help you get started with using AI whilst building Drupal sites.</p>
<p>Jamie is a co-founder of a Drupal agency called FreelyGive and is obsessed with AI. Though not a programmer, he has been involved with the founding of the AI module, the AI Initiative and will come and share with you the history of AI in Drupal, what you can do today and where things are going next.</p>

### The Web Index : The Invisible Layer AI Runs On

- Date: 1 December 2026
- Time: 17:00–17:25
- Room: Acacia 3/4
- Conference: GenerationAI
- Track: Fine-Tuning & Web Data
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Guillaume Pitel

<p>Everyone is talking about LLMs. Almost no one is talking about what they’re built on: the web index. Behind every AI-generated answer lies a system that discovers, crawls, cleans, and indexes billions of pages, and today only a handful of such indexes still exist in the world.</p>
<p>This talk goes under the hood. We’ll start with a short history of search: from text matching to links, from meaning to direct answers. Each breakthrough changed the interface, but the foundation never moved: crawl, index, query. We’ll then walk through how a web index actually works, from discovery and parsing to quality signals, retrieval, and ranking, and explain why crawling well is one of the hardest problems in computing.</p>
<p>Finally, we’ll look at what’s at stake. LLMs haven’t replaced search engines; they depend on them. The web’s original economic contract, traffic in exchange for content, is breaking down as synthesized answers keep users from ever clicking through. And Europe, despite an ambitious regulatory framework (GDPR, DMA, AI Act), has no index of its own to enforce it.</p>
<p>Whoever controls the index controls what AI can see, cite, and say. This talk explains why that invisible layer deserves far more attention than it gets, and why it matters for anyone building, regulating, or relying on AI.</p>

### Opening Remarks

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 09:30–09:55
- Room: Acacia 4
- Conference: OpenLLM Europe
- Type: Opening remarks
- Speakers: Julie Hunter, Michel-Marie Maudet

### What the history of AI tells us about its future

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 09:30–09:55
- Room: Amphitheater
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: Keynotes
- Type: Keynote
- Speakers: Alexandre Gefen

<p>Today’s AI inherits two things from its past: a set of myths and a set of unsolved scientific questions. This 20-minute talk first walks through seven figures that predate the computer and still shape products, benchmarks and fears: the speaking statue of Amun (religion), Vaucanson’s duck (rivalling nature), the chess automaton (play), the Jaquet-Droz writer (art), Pygmalion (love), Talos (war) and the Golem (fear). It then follows six periods, from the calculating machines of the 17th century to the foundation models of 2018-2025, through cybernetics, the Dartmouth moment, the first winter, expert systems and the return of neural networks. Each stage ends with a short “looking ahead” box asking what it may suggest for the systems being built now, and the talk closes on five hypotheses rather than predictions. One more thing: “vintage” language models trained only on texts of the past, and FLAUBOT, a Flaubert chatbot, serve as a demonstration that tools built from the past can be used to test our ideas about the future.
The talk draws on Histoire culturelle de l’IA (CNRS Éditions, 2026).</p>

### AI and planetary boundaries

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 09:30–10:00
- Room: Dragonnier 6
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Keynote
- Speakers: Philippe Bihouix

<p>Techno-optimist promises are more pervasive than ever. If we are to believe the &quot;prophets&quot; of Silicon Valley, artificial intelligence will &quot;fix&quot; the climate and lead to a future of phenomenal abundance and wealth for all – driven by autonomous robots, neural chips and the conquest of space. Is this truly realistic – or even sensible –, given planetary boundaries, environmental degradation and widespread pollution, fast-growing demands for resources and energy, and geopolitical tensions? What if our energy transition and innovation efforts were to focus on different paths – ones that are maybe more &quot;restrained&quot; but far more resilient?</p>

### Thinking With Machines

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 10:00–10:25
- Room: Amphitheater
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: Keynotes
- Type: Keynote
- Speakers: Mike Amundsen

<p>What is the future of human thinking in an AI-native world? As intelligent machines become part of how we work, design, and make decisions, how can we use them to expand human capability and creativity?
Thinking with Machines revisits a longstanding idea in computing: machines should help people reason, explore, and decide, rather than simply automate outcomes. Building on the work of Vannevar Bush, J. C. R. Licklider, and Douglas Engelbart, this keynote explores what it means to design AI-native systems that treat machines as active partners in human reasoning.
Through engaging stories and practical examples, the talk introduces patterns for AI-guided exploration, collaborative problem-solving, and decision support. It shows how thoughtful interaction design can extend human judgment and strengthen creative thinking while preserving human agency.
The result is a practical vision for AI-native computing grounded in decades of research on human-machine collaboration and focused on making modern AI useful in complex, real-world work.</p>

<p>In this talk, I will present the open LLM and AI activities at the Barcelona Supercomputing Center (BSC). I will introduce our pragmatic approach to AI sovereignty, highlighting the importance of building capabilities across the AI value chain, from infrastructure and model development to deployment and real-world adoption. I will overview our ongoing and future initiatives in open AI, including ALIA, AINA, and OpenEuroLLM, as well as the use cases and applications we are developing with industry and public-sector partners. I will also discuss the role of the BSC AI Factory (AIF) in bridging research and innovation with practical AI adoption, providing access to expertise, infrastructure, and support for the development and deployment of AI solutions. ### Your API platform was built for humans. Nobody told the agents

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 10:30–10:55
- Room: Amphitheater
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: Keynotes
- Type: Keynote
- Speakers: Harry Ault

<p>Every product team wants its MCP exposed to agents. Every BU wants access to SaaS MCPs. They all want it this quarter. The problem isn’t that your API team needs to become a platform team. You made that shift years ago. The problem is that your platform assumes consumers who read docs, onboard once, authenticate as an app, and call at human speed. Agents break every one of those assumptions, and adding people won’t close the gap. This keynote covers what has to move from manual process into the platform itself: MCP servers generated from existing APIs, governance in the CI pipeline, an identity for every agent, and policy enforced at the gateway. It draws on how Choreo Labs, formerly WSO2, customers in 90+ countries run 60 trillion transactions a year without a review meeting for each one.</p>

### Trusting the “Green” in Tech: How ISO 14064 Third-Party Verification Combats Greenwashing

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 10:35–11:00
- Room: Dragonnier 6
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Melanie Blackmore

<p>Tech and AI companies are publishing carbon numbers, but a published number isn’t a credible one. The European Commission found 53% of green claims were vague, misleading, or unfounded. With ECGT applying since September 2026 and CSRD, UK SRS and SB 253 expecting assured data, self-reported emissions are a growing liability. This talk explains how independent verification under ISO 14064-3 works, common data gaps, and how to make emissions data audit-ready and reusable across disclosures. It also asks where and how AI can ease verification and where human independence must remain.</p>

<p>Developer Experience (DX) has long become a critical success factor for API initiatives. Whether you are providing or consuming APIs: friction in development slows adoption, increases support effort, and in the worst case can cause integrations to fail.
In this talk, we focus on what DX means, especially in the context of APIs and how DX for providers and DX for consumers are linked. Based on Team Topologies, we explore how a platform team can improve DX for both API providers and consumers. This includes topics such as consistent design, documentation, maintenance, API discovery, and the lifecycle of APIs.
API Developer Experience is not a &quot;&quot;nice to have&quot;&quot;, but a strategic lever for platform growth and the success of the ecosystem.</p>

### Public Procurement of Open Source for Energy Transition

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:20–11:45
- Room: Dattier 3
- Conference: Digital Resilience Europe
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Boris Dolley

<p>Every year, European public procurement totals over €2,500 billion in spending, with approximately €120 billion dedicated to the digital needs of European public entities. Historically, we've adopted a commodity software approach, renting these services primarily from American vendors — to whom European organizations now transfer €330 billion annually, 80% of all professional cloud and software spending. A recent Asterès study commissioned by CIGREF quantifies the cost of this dependency: €140 billion per year in excess costs by 2030, with €93 billion leaving the European economy entirely, destroying an estimated 1.4 million jobs. RTE's OSPO presentation demonstrates the power of a coordinated public procurement strategy across an industrial vertical to change this dynamic. We're now actively investing in the digital assets of the energy transition through Open Source. Digital service companies, foundations, private procurement, startups, and SMEs all have opportunities to capture value worth tens, then hundreds, of billions of euros — if we mobilize together across all industrial verticals through Open Source.</p>

### Streaming's Energy Use. Watts, Methods, and What the Industry Needs to Measure Next

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:20–11:45
- Room: Davidia 1
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Benjamin Schwarz

<p>Most streaming energy figures are modeled. Greening of Streaming spent 4 years measuring instead: real devices, codecs, and traffic, metered at the socket. Across a 128-fold bitrate range, a streaming box moved under half a watt while the TV drew ~50 W. We cover where the watts go, the methodology that makes numbers defensible, and open questions: is per-GB network energy a measure of efficiency or just pipe fill? Why do some devices draw significantly more with newer codecs? Where should the home boundary sit? Green IO attendees will see a new GoS dataset first.</p>

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:20–11:45
- Room: Dragonnier 6
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: James Martin

<p>We know the theory. Smaller AI models are not only less impactful; you can also host them wherever you want, and keep them and their data for as long as you want. So they're great for sustainability and sovereignty; not to mention hundreds of times cheaper, or even free if run locally. But how many companies really care about making their AI usage more frugal and sovereign? More than you think! BetterTech's James Martin shares real-life insights gained since launching his &quot;Frugal AI&quot; training course, with GreenIT.fr, in September 2025.</p>

### Your Agents Are Running the Organisation ,Who's Watching Them?

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:20–11:45
- Room: Aubépine 3
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: API observabilty and testing for Agents
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Kelly Policarpo

<p>AI agents have stopped assisting and started operating. They run workflows, make decisions, and execute tasks that used to require a person. In many organisations, humans are quietly becoming the minority in functions they once owned outright. This talk examines what governance looks like once autonomous AI becomes the workforce, not just the tool. Drawing on European regulatory frameworks and experience shipping AI systems in HR Tech, Legal Tech, and Health Tech, I introduce a practical framework for what human presence an organisation still needs: who holds authority, who's accountable, where humans still participate, and what continuity requires when today's process owners may not be there tomorrow.</p>
<p>Why it matters:</p>
<p>Most agentic AI conversations focus on capability. Almost none focus on governance, on what breaks when agents act without anyone watching. Teams shipping agents into production are answering the &quot;&quot;who's in charge&quot;&quot; question today, often without realising it. This talk gives builders and product leaders a concrete lens to audit their own systems before a regulator, customer, or incident forces the question.</p>

### Your Next API Consumer Is Not Human: Designing APIs for AI Agents

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:20–11:45
- Room: Aubépine 2
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: API Design for AI
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Hussein Loubani

<p>Your next API consumer is not a developer reading your documentation but an AI agent that picks a tool, fills in the parameters on its own, and acts on whatever your API returns without a human in between. Most APIs were never designed for this kind of caller, and the cracks show fast.</p>
<p>I learned this the hard way while building agentic AI systems and connecting my research workflows through the Model Context Protocol. Whenever the agent failed, the model was rarely the problem, because the real issue lived in the API: a vague tool description sent it to the wrong endpoint, a loose schema let it guess instead of failing cleanly, and an error written for humans gave it nothing to recover from.</p>
<p>This talk shows what changes when the consumer is a machine, and why it matters now: agents are already calling your APIs whether you planned for them or not. You leave with a practical checklist for making yours agent-ready, drawn from real failures rather than vendor slides.</p>

<p>In this talk, I will introduce CulturalRiddles, a community-built benchmark of human-written riddles spanning 62 cultures and 51 languages. ### From API Guidelines to Governance Skills

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:50–12:15
- Room: Aubépine 4
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: APIs Governance, Ecosystems and Platforms
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Daniel Kocot

<p>API governance rarely fails because organizations lack guidelines. It fails because those guidelines are often too far away from the daily work of service teams. They exist in documents, review meetings, and internal portals, but they are not always present when API design decisions are actually made.
This talk builds on spec driven API design and looks at how Skills can help organizations bring API governance closer to development. Instead of treating API guidelines as static documentation, teams can turn design principles, naming conventions, review criteria, security expectations, and domain standards into reusable Skills that support developers while APIs are being designed and evolved.
The session shows how Skills can assist with OpenAPI reviews, resource naming, consistent error models, breaking change awareness, and better design feedback. The goal is not to replace architects, API platform teams, or governance boards. The goal is to make their knowledge available earlier, more consistently, and closer to the workflow where API quality is created.**
The talk closes with a practical model for moving from static guidelines to living governance capabilities, and with a critical reminder: Skills only become governance when they have ownership, versioning, feedback loops, and accountability.</p>

### From API Traffic to Agent Execution: Why AI Agents Need a Different Gateway

- Date: 2 December 2026
- Time: 11:50–12:15
- Room: Aubépine 1
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: AI Gateways and Orchestration
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Gbadebo Bello

<p>AI agents do not behave like traditional API clients. They choose tools dynamically, call models and services in changing sequences, carry context across steps, and can retry or act autonomously. That makes the familiar request-by-request controls of an API gateway necessary, but no longer sufficient.</p>
<p>This talk explores the shift from managing API traffic to governing agent execution. We'll look at the identity, authorization, tool discovery, context, reliability, cost, and observability challenges that emerge when agents work across LLMs, MCP servers, APIs, and other agents. Then, through a live Banking investigation demo, we'll compare a direct agent integration with a gateway-mediated path to show what changes when control is applied across the whole execution flow, not just at a single endpoint.</p>
