<p>The recent development of agentic based systems have grown the LLM usage by orders of magnitude. This growth has been measurable at the macro level with the deployment of datacenters full of GPUs, but at the local level, developers have few options to understand the impact of using tools such as Claude Code or other coding agents.
During this workshop we will see how the agentic-footprint package combines the estimation of remote APIs using EcoLogits, with CodeCarbon to measure the consumption of local runtime of those agents and monitor those local use cases with popular coding agents.</p>

### Do you really need an LLM for that? How Orange rethinks AI efficiency.

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 15:55–16:20
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Romain Carbou, Rose Djagbre

<p>Token consumption by apps is skyrocketing, leading to significant costs for both the planet and your margins. Sometimes, the answer to &quot;Do you really need an LLM for that?&quot; is simply no. That’s why we built a smart LLM proxy: it goes beyond mere monitoring, observes LLM calls without interfering, uncovers recurring patterns that could be handled by lighter approaches, and maintains a library of resource-efficient ML pipelines. It then recommends these alternatives and conducts live A/B tests, comparing quality, cost, lifecycle impact against the LLM. With an open-source mindset in mind.</p>

### GENIA - Testing - Industrialiser la QA logicielle avec une architecture Multi-Agents.

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 15:55–16:20
- Room: Aubépine 1
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: API talks in french
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Betty Beauge, Gilles Nouais

<p>Avec l'IA générative, les développeurs codent plus vite. Le corollaire ? Le risque de générer de la dette technique et des anomalies à grande vitesse. Comment l'Assurance Qualité (QA) peut-elle tenir ce rythme industriel sans sacrifier la rigueur des standards ?
Cette conférence présente notre retour d'expérience terrain au sein d'une Testing Factory, à travers &quot;&quot;QA Forge&quot;&quot; : un framework DevSecOps où l'IA a été industrialisée. Je vous présenterai comment nous sommes passés du simple &quot;&quot;prompt&quot;&quot; à une architecture orchestrant 17 agents IA spécialisés sur la qualité.
De l'analyse &quot;&quot;Shift-Left&quot;&quot; d'une User Story Jira (critères INVEST, risques) à la génération de code automatisé (Playwright pour l'UI, Bruno pour les API), l'IA intervient à chaque étape. Mais le véritable enjeu abordé sera la confiance : comment éviter les hallucinations ? Nous détaillerons notre approche &quot;&quot;Supervised LLM&quot;&quot; (où une IA évaluatrice audite le travail de l'IA productrice) et notre infrastructure &quot;&quot;Agents as Code&quot;&quot; utilisant Cursor, le protocole MCP et Git comme source de vérité.
Cette présentation démontre concrètement comment l'IA ne remplace pas la QA, mais la transforme. Le testeur devient un &quot;&quot;Testeur Augmenté&quot;&quot;, architecte et garant d'une qualité logicielle générée &quot;&quot;by design&quot;&quot;.</p>

### The European Techno-Democracy Coalition

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 15:55–16:20
- Room: Dragonnier 4
- Conference: Digital Resilience Europe
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Elisa Lironi

<p>Technology now shapes how we speak, learn, work and vote. Yet the rules that govern it are increasingly written by a handful of powerful companies and governments, far from the citizens whose lives it shapes. How can Europe make sure that technology serves democracy, rather than the other way round? This talk will present the European Techno-Democracy Coalition, a multi-stakeholder initiative convened by the European Citizen Action Service (ECAS) and co-funded by the European Union’s CERV programme. It introduces the Coalition’s Techno-Democracy Blueprint, a framework built on three layers: the values technology must uphold, the institutions needed to guarantee them, and the design principles that turn them into everyday products. Drawing on the collaborative process behind the Blueprint (that include 24 articles developed by around 30 academics, NGOs and European tech companies), the talk explores concrete answers to some of today’s most pressing challenges. These include concentration of power, data exploitation, addictive design and the opacity of AI. It concludes with the Coalition’s European campaign running up to the next European Elections 2029, inviting citizens and organisations to support a democratic vision for Europe’s digital future.</p>

### Build creative, interactive apps & games

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 15:55–16:30
- Room: Dragonnier 3
- Conference: Build It Summit Paris 2026
- Type: Panel discussion
- Speakers: Pierre Lacombe, Timothée Le Borgne, Cedric Giorgi

<p>AI Builders are no longer limited to forms and dashboards. Today you can go from one sentence to an interactive app, a story or a game, and even run it on AI that works locally, without a network connection. OhMyUnicorn and Celestory each open with a 5 to 7 minute demo, then discuss what changes when anyone can build interactive experiences: who builds them, what they are used for, and where the limits still are.</p>

### Your Cloud May be Sovereign. But Is Your Network?

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 16:05–16:20
- Room: Dattier 3
- Conference: SUPERstack
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Federico Capoano

<p>As European organizations move toward sovereign cloud infrastructure, one critical component is often overlooked: the network itself. Routers, access points, and network management systems frequently remain tied to proprietary technologies, vendor-controlled platforms, and external cloud services. This talk explores why network independence matters, the challenges of escaping vendor lock-in, and how open-source technologies like OpenWrt and OpenWISP can help organizations regain control of their network infrastructure.</p>

### How to get people on board with responsible tech ? From awareness to engagement

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 16:15–16:40
- Room: Davidia 1
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Workshop
- Speakers: Jérôme Vigogne, Sandra Sydow

<p>We'll share with you key insights on how to improve people's engagement on adopting responsible tech practices. At Planet On Stage, with more than 6 years of experience in running awareness programmes in companies, universities and communities worldwide, we have managed to engage 25k+ people on the difficult topics related to environmental challenges. How did we achieve this? Combining powerful visual storytelling and collective experiences through our unique Digital Impact Pitch format, with Communication with Impact Masterclasses that equip people with the soft skills to inspire change. Join us to discover how to make responsible tech everyone’s business!</p>

### Fair & Responsible AI for Sustainability: Governance, Resilience and Adoption

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 16:20–16:45
- Room: Dragonnier 6
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Louis de Diesbach

<p>Every organisation is rolling out AI, but few can answer: AI, sure - but to do what? Too often adopted as an end rather than a means, AI brings runaway costs, governance blind spots, and a growing environmental footprint.
This talk offers a practitioner's roadmap for responsible AI adoption: an AI vision anchored in your mission, a governance ecosystem across four pillars (strategy, risk, support, continuous improvement), and three practical starting points - trustworthy data, a first AI policy, training people. We'll close with the questions that matter: what do we do with the productivity</p>

### Une IA sobre qui doit tout prouver

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 16:20–16:45
- Room: Dragonnier 5
- Conference: GreenIO
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Jérémy Pastouret

<p>Personne ne lit les politiques de confidentialité. Nous avons voulu en faire lire 134 à une IA, et les questions sont arrivées avant les réponses. Tout confier au modèle, ou seulement une partie ? Est-ce qu'un simple algorithme, ou des statistiques, font aussi bien sans IA ? Comment savoir quand le modèle invente ? Combien d'appels sont vraiment nécessaires ? Que reste-t-il aux humains ? RETEX sur les choix faits, nos erreurs, nos succès et ce qu'un usage sobre de l'IA veut dire concrètement.</p>

### De 6 mois à 2 jours : la révolution LLM pour le traitement documentaire

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 16:25–16:50
- Room: Aubépine 1
- Conference: Apidays
- Track: API talks in french
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Vinorth Varatharasan

<p>Un nouveau document arrive, un fournisseur change son template, une réglementation impose un nouveau champ : et c’est reparti pour un tour de réannotation et de réentraînement. Pour une équipe qui gère des dizaines de types de documents, ce cycle ne s’arrête jamais, mobilisant plusieurs ingénieurs ML pendant des mois pour un gain marginal.</p>
<p>Ce cycle vient du deep learning classique, qui doit réapprendre chaque mise en page avant de pouvoir la lire. Les LLM multimodaux savent déjà lire un document. Le travail change de nature : on n’entraîne plus de modèles sur de gros GPU, mais on configure un LLM avec un prompt, un schéma de sortie et des règles métier.</p>
<p>À partir d’un cas réel de vérification d’identité bancaire (KYC) et d’une démo live, nous verrons comment passer d’un POC en deux jours à un système en production. Le prompt devient le nouveau modèle, mais chaque sortie doit être validée avant qu’on lui fasse confiance, et un humain reste en charge des cas douteux.</p>

### External Communications: A Critical Component of Digital Resilience

- Date: 3 December 2026
- Time: 16:25–16:50
- Room: Dattier 3
- Conference: SUPERstack
- Type: Talk
- Speakers: Emily Omier

<p>How do you get the word out to constituents when something important is happening in your community? Whether it’s a city government or a community of artists, the answer is often dependent on American tech companies. We talk a lot about how governments, companies and organizations of all kinds can continue their internal functions if they are cut off from American tech companies, but often neglect the conversation about how to maintain contact with the public while maintaining digital independence. In this talk, Emily Omier will talk about how Mastodon gives organizations of all types the freedom to connect with their audiences independently, and the advantages both for digital resilience and user experience.</p>
